2. RECOGNITION 
A. TSD Mission Moment – Athens Photography Students 
Athens’ Visual Arts teacher Luba Sordyl is here with us this evening to share 
a little about an idea her Photography 2 students came up with and decided 
to put into action to help out students in their own community.

certificates to students.

 2. B. 11.19.19 
2. RECOGNITION 
B. 2019 Athens Boys’ Soccer State Champs 
Director of Athletics, Mr. Tim Fulcher, is here with us this evening to announce 
the 2019 Athens Boys Soccer  Team State Champs and tell us about their 
successful season. 
Recommended Action:  Invite Mr. Fulcher up to introduce the team and 
share their victories.   
Tim will read: 
The team was led by captains Davis DiGiovanni, Jason Kemp, Andri Myftari  and 
Donovan Sharp.  The team was ranked #1 or #2 throughout the entire regular 
season; they also spent some time in the national rankings.    During the season, 
the Red Hawks collected 5 trophies  -   a Soccer City Cup, an OAA Red Division 
Championship, a District Championship, a Regional Championship and a MHSAA 
Division One State Championship.  Athens Soccer had 6 players receive All-League 
Honors, 8 with All-District Honors, 4 with All-Region Honors and 4 with All-State 
Honors.  They finished the season ranked #1 in Michigan and #13 by the United 
Soccer Coaches.

Youngstrom  reviewed  gov ernmental  revenue  and

Memorandum 
  TO: Dr. Rich Machesky, Superintendent of Schools 
Office 
4400 Livernois 
Troy, MI 48098 
Fax: 248.823.4013 
  RE: Policy Update Considerations for Policy Committee  
   DATE: Thursday, November 7, 2019 
On Wednesday, October 23, 2019, the Policy Committee of the Board of Education reviewed the 
policy updates from Lusk and Albertson and recommend the following policy additions/changes be 
adopted by the Board of Education: 
1. To clear any confusion regarding which Board members are subject to the “Conflict 
of Interest” policy, typically found under Section 1001/1000.01, we recommend 
the following underlined change: 
Conflict of Interest. If a member of the immediate family (father, mother, 
husband, wife, son, daughter, sister, brother, father-in-law, mother-in-law, son-
in-law, daughter-in-law, sister-in-law, brother-in-law, or step-family members) 
of a newly elected Board member is  already an employee of the School 
District, such Board member shall abstain from voting on any matter affecting 
the employment status of the employee. 
2. We recommend the following underlined change to the first and last sentences 
of “Curriculum Development,” typically found under Section 3001/3000.01, to 
comply with MCL 380.1282(2): 
Board directs the Superintendent is responsible for the to development, 
implementation, and provide ongoing evaluation of the School District’s 
curriculum. The curriculum will: 
 Be consistent with the Board’s policy on Student Learning and 
Achievement; 
 Meet or exceed all requirements of the State of Michigan for 
instructional programs; and, 
 Be standards- based and founded upon legally compliant, research- 
based grade level learning and achievement standards that lead to 
the awarding of a School District diploma. 
The School District’s curriculum will also include legally compliant, research - 
based learning  and achievement standards for students who participate in 
career and technical education programs, as well as address the needs and 
provide legally  compliant opportunities for students with disabilities and 
students who are considered gifted. 
26
  FROM: Rick West, Assistant Superintendent, Business Services
  CC: Judy Holmes 
Business 
Phone: 248.823.4022 
The Superintendent will appoint well qualified administrators and teachers to 
assist the Superintendent in implementing and improving the School District’s 
curriculum, as well as otherwise improving student learning and achievement. 
Any changes to the curriculum must be approved by the Board. 
3. The federal Drug -Free Workplace Act, 41 USC 8101, et seq. requires all school 
districts to establish an employee drug  awareness program in exchange for certain 
federal funding.  To that end, we recommend addition of the following underlined 
language to “Alcohol and Drug Free Workplace,” typically found under Section 
4003/4000.03:   
Alcohol and Drug Free Workplace the Bo ard maintains a workplace free of 
alcohol and illegal drugs, as well as prescription drugs for which the employee 
does not have a current, valid prescription.  An employee or volunteer who is 
found to have unlawfully manufactured, distributed, dispensed, p ossessed, or 
used alcohol or any drug in the  workplace shall be disciplined, up to and 
including discharge from employment.  Similarly, an employee or volunteer who 
is found to have been present in the workplace while under the influence of 
illegal drugs, prescription drugs for which the employee does not have a current, 
valid prescription, or alcohol will be subject to discipline, up to and including 
discharge from employment.  All employees must notify the School District, in 
writing, if charged with a vi olation of a criminal drug statute occurring in the 
workplace.  Notification must be provided no more than three (3) business days 
after being arraigned for the crime.  All employees agree to abide by this policy 
as a condition of employment.  This notification requirement does not eliminate 
the obligation of School District employees or volunteers to report convictions 
of felonies and/or listed offenses, as otherwise required by law.  
Notwithstanding state permissibility, the use and possession of marijuana and 
marijuana-induced intoxication are prohibited on school grounds.   
The School District’s Assistant Superintendent, Employee Services is directed 
to establish a drug -free awarene ss program to inform employees about: the 
dangers of drug abuse in the workplace; employee awareness of this policy; and 
available counseling, rehabilitation, and employee assistance programs. 
4. In December 2018, Michigan enacted the Paid Medical Leave Act , MCL 408.961, 
et seq.  Briefly, the Act requires employers to provide accrued paid medical leave to 
use for employees’ (or employees’ family members’) medical needs or for purposes 
related to domestic violence and sexual assault.  To comply with the law, we 
recommend addition of the following policy, to be placed between “FMLA” and 
“Medical Examinations,” typically found under Section 4003/4000.03: 
Paid Medical Leave . The School District will provide paid medical leave 
(PML) to eligible employees pursuant to Michigan law.  The Superintendent will 
determine whether PML will accrue over the course of each benefit year or will 
be provided at the beginning of each benefit year and will promulgate 
administrative regulations concerning the use of PML.  Payment and use of 
accrued or provided PML will be coordinated with all types of paid leave 
available to an eligible employee pursuant to collective bargaining agreements, 
individual contracts, or other School District policies and administrative 
27
regulations.  This policy and any implementing administrative regulations will 
be automatically rescinded, without further action by the Board or the 
Superintendent, if paid medical leave is mandated by federal law. 
5. We recommend the following underlined change to the “Medical 
Examinations” subheading, typically found under 4003/4000.03, to comply with 
federal law: 
Medical Examinations. The Superintendent or his/her designee may require an 
employee to submit to a medical examination when: 
 Required or permitted by federal or state law. 
 Required or permitted by the employee’s contract of 
employment, to the extent permitted by law. 
 Information suggests that a health condition may be negatively 
affecting the employee’s ability to perform the essential functions of 
his/her job, with or without accommodations. 
 Information suggests that the employee is a direct threat to his/her 
safety or the safety of others. 
 An employee has provided insufficient medical documentation as 
the basis for a health leave or in return from a health leave and, after 
providing the employee  an opportunity to supplement the 
documentation, the School District believes that  a second medical 
opinion is warranted  documentation remains insufficient. 
If the Superintendent requires an employee to submit to a medical examination, 
all costs will be borne by the School District. The employee will be required 
to sign a release authorizing the physician to submit a copy of the report of the 
examination directly to  the Superintendent. A copy of the physician’s report 
will be maintained in a separate, confidential personnel file. 
6. Governor Whitmer signed into law House Bill 4242 which amends certain sections  
of the State School Aid Act. One of those sections is MCL 388.1766, which, 
generally, prohibits school districts from referring students for abortions or assisting 
students in obtaining abortions. The statute also: required school district personnel 
to provide sex education in compliance with the Revised School Code and assessed 
a financial penalty against any school district official, employee, or Board member 
who referred or assisted a student in obtaining an abortion or violated MCL 380.1507. 
The recent amendments remove references to the Revised School Code and to 
any financial penalties assessed against school district personnel. In addition, school 
districts will no  longer forfeit $100,000 in state aid funding for failure to adopt a 
policy consistent with MCL  388.1766; instead, school districts may be subject to a 
forfeiture of 1% of state aid funding if corrective action is not taken under MCL 
3800.1766a. The amendments also added an exception to the general prohibition 
of referral or assistance language, by excluding employees, Board members, and 
officials who are parents of students. 
28
In light of these amendments, we recommend the following, underlined changes to the 
“Prohibition of Referral or Assistance” policy, typically found under Section 
4005/4000.05: 
Prohibition of Referral or Assistance. All District employees, Board members, 
and school officials must provide instruction in sex education in strict compliance 
with MCL 380.1507 of the Revised School Code. In addition, All District 
employees, Board members, and school officials are prohibited from referring a 
student for an abortion or assisting a student in obtaining an abortion. This policy 
does not apply to employees, Board members,  and school officials who are 
parents or legal guardians of that student. 
Pursuant to state law, District personnel found to be in violation of this policy 
will be assessed a fin e by the District of no less than 3% of his or her annual 
compensation. The District will refund the State School Aid Fund in an amount 
equal to any fines applied  to or collected from District personnel under this  
policy. 
7. Recently, MDE approved a new Michigan Code of Educational Ethics for all  
individuals working with and for students within the state. To that end, we 
recommend addition of the following subheading at the end of “Other Matters of 
Employment,” typically found as Section 4005/4000.05: 
Codes of Ethics. The School District expects all individuals working with 
or for its students to comply with all applicable professional codes of eth ics, 
including the Michigan Code of Educational Ethics , as approved by the 
Michigan Department of Education. 
8. We recommend a small change to “Budget Planning and Adoption,” typically found 
under 5002/5000.02. This change acknowledges the former Governmental 
Accounting  Standards Board (GASB) Statement No. 54 was superseded by GASB 
84. However, instead of including a  specific GASB number, we recommend 
including the following underlined language which  will ensure any future GASB 
statement supersessions will not require an update to your policy manual: 
The Board is required to adopt a budget prior to July 1 of each year, to be in 
place for the ensuing school year.  The budget is a formalized statement of 
anticipated revenues and expenditures of the School District and includes all 
District fund categories that are  used to  carry-out the School District's 
educational goals and objectives. The budget will be prepared and published 
in conformity with the most recent, applicable GASB 54 requirements.  The 
Board will endeavor to maintain a minimum year -end general fund balance of 
fifteen (15) percent.  
The Superintendent is responsible for preparing the budget and its timely 
presentation to the  Board. Following adoption by the Board, the 
Superintendent will regularly inform the Board of actual or anticipated 
variances that may occur during budget  implementation and recommend any 
action that may be required to be taken by the Board. 
29
9. We recommend the small underlined change to the “Fixed Assets” policy, 
typically found under 5010/5000.10: 
The Board shall maintain a fixed asset procedure sufficient to track applicable 
purchases and donations of items meeting the following criteria: 
 The cost of each item shall exceed $5,000; 
 The item shall have an estimated useful life which exceeds one 
year; and/or 
 Items costing less than $5,000 each may also be included within 
this policy if such item is deemed to be theft prone or otherwise 
warranting tracking. 
Fixed assets shall be classified into the following major categories: 
 Land: 
 Buildings; 
 Vehicles; 
 Improvements other than buildings; 
 Construction in Progress; and 
 Machinery and Equipment. 
The Superintendent shall develop such procedures and practices sufficient to 
meet this Policy. 
10. A number of school districts have recently expressed concern over the lack of 
knowledge they have surrounding the criminal history of people who volunteer at 
their schools. To rectify this concern, we recommend addition of the following 
“Volunteers” policy at the end of the 7000 Series. Please note your district has 
two options with respect to how frequently background checks are performed on 
a single volunteer: 
The Board encourages the use of volunteers to support the School District. 
Because the safety of the School District’s students is of paramount 
importance, every individual volunteer is required to undergo a criminal 
background check through the Michigan State Police Internet Criminal History 
Access Tool (ICHAT) before s/he is permitted to volunteer,  regardless of 
whether or not the volunteer will work directly with students. Volunteers may 
be required to undergo new ICHAT criminal background checks at the 
discretion of the School. 
No individual will be permitted to volunteer if  s/he has been convicted of a  
misdemeanor described in, or a felony that is a “listed offense” as defined in, 
MCL 380.1535a or  MCL 380.1539b. If an individual has been convicted of a 
felony that is not a listed offense,  as defined in MCL 380.1535a or MCL 
30
380.1539b, the individual may only be permitted  to volunteer if the Board and 
the Superintendent both approve the volunteer assignment in writing. This policy 
does not grant any individual who passes an ICHAT background check with the 
right to be approved as a volunteer. 
In addition to these recommendations, a Board member requested at the last Board 
of Education Meeting that they discuss a policy on use of electronic devices during 
open meetings.  We may want to consider adding this conversation to the agenda of 
the Policy Committee.

The collective bargaining agreement between the Troy School District and 
the TEA/MEA/NEA expires on January 31,2020. This union represents the 
teaching staff in the Troy School District.

the Board in upcoming negotiations with the TEA/MEA/NEA, whose contract expires 
on January 31, 2020.

agreement with this Association.

Memorandum 
 TO:       Rick West - Asst. Supt. Business & Finance 
Operations 
 FROM:  Rob Carson – Director of Maintenance & Operations   
Department 
Troy, MI 48083 
Ph: 248.823.4050 
A RFP (Public Bid 9892) was issued to obtain competitive bids for roof replacement work 
at Athens, International Academy East & Troy High  Schools and roof restoration work at 
Barnard, Costello & Martell Elementary Schools along with the Services Building .  The 
scope of work for this RFP is to provide complete roof replacement and roof restoration to 
roof areas at the specified schools that were identified as priorities through our Roof Asset 
Management Plan.  
The RFP resulted in 7 bids; JD Candler and Royal West were each the low qualified bidder 
for the following schools: 
JD Candler – Total Recommended Award $1,469,500.00 
Athens – Roof Area G 
IAE – Roof Areas B, C, D, E & F 
Barnard, Costello, Martell & Services – Restorative work as specified 
Royal West Roofing – Total Recommended Award $740,000.00 
Troy High – Roof Areas A, B, C, D 
All bids were thoroughly reviewed , the low bidders were interviewed to evaluate their 
proposals for thoroughness and accuracy.  JD Candler and Royal West have both 
successfully performed similar work for the Troy School Distric t. The Contractor Bid 
Recommendation by Weather Tech and Bid Summary is attached for complete bid results 
and support of this recommendation. 
The total amount of roofing work to be completed in 201 9 is $2,319,975.00 including a 
requested 5% Contingency. A separate Purchase Order will be issued for J.D. Candler  & 
Royal West Roofing. The funding for these purchases will be from the Capitol Projects 
Fund. 
If you have any questions, please let me know.  
Cc:   Pat Orczykowski – Business Department  
 Purchasing Department – File
